**Step 1:**  
White’s testimony (Testimony 2) says *“Miss Mia run to the left!”* — Mia fled left after being attacked.  
Evidence 9 (May Testimony) states *“The victim dodged an attack then ran to the right, but she was caught and struck.”* — Mia fled right. These two accounts describe opposite directions of movement, which cannot both be true for the same event.

**Step 2:**  
The listed spatial propositions (1‑10) concern room co‑location, visibility, fingerprint hand usage, etc. None of them address directional movement (left vs. right), so no proposition directly applies.

**Step 3:**  
Since no proposition applies, we rely on basic spatial reasoning: an object (Mia) cannot simultaneously move left and right at the same moment. The contradictory directional claims constitute the inconsistency.

**Step 4:**  
The contradiction is identified by comparing the spatial description of Mia’s motion in the two sources. One source places her moving left, the other places her moving right; these are mutually exclusive.

{"evidence": 9, "testimony": 2}

The ladder provides an alternative exit, violating the “only exit” statement.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is identified by comparing the spatial claim in the testimony (single exit) with visual evidence of a second exit (ladder) within the same space, which the listed propositions do not account for.

**Step 3:** Because the bullet hole is located in the boarding lounge, the shot that created it must have occurred there, not in the Control Room. This directly conflicts with Cosmos’s claim that the figure fired at them while they were in the Control Room.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is identified by comparing the location of the physical evidence (boarding lounge) with the location asserted in the testimony (Control Room). Since a single shot cannot simultaneously originate in two separate rooms, the evidence and testimony cannot both be true.

Will produce explanation then final JSON line.



**Step 1:**  
Testimony 3 (by Nosa) states: *“The black‑uniformed varsity cadet fired on the English civilian! **And from the back**, the cowardly little weasel!”* – Nosa is claiming the shooter fired from **behind** the victim.  

Evidence 2 (Photograph of Victim) shows the victim was **shot from the front** in the chest.

These two statements cannot both be true: the bullet cannot simultaneously originate from the front and from the back.

**Step 2:**  
Relevant spatial propositions:  

- **Prop 1** – “The victim was shot from the front, therefore the bullet came from the front.” (supports Evidence 2)  
- **Prop 5** – “Nosa claimed the shooter fired from the back, implying the victim was hit from behind and therefore the victim fell forward.” (supports Testimony 3)  

**Step 3:**  
Prop 1 and Prop 5 directly conflict: Prop 1 asserts a front‑originating shot, while Prop 5 asserts a back‑originating shot. Because the two propositions are mutually exclusive, the evidence (front shot) and the testimony (back shot) contradict each other.

**Step 4:**  
Even without the explicit propositions, spatial reasoning tells us that a single bullet can only travel along one line of attack. A photograph confirming a frontal wound cannot be reconciled with an eyewitness claim of a rearward shot.

**Conclusion:** The contradiction is between **Evidence 2** and **Testimony 3**.
